**Session 2026-09-08 (cont'd, 50th pass) — Closed out the 49th pass's two flagged-not-fixed items (Ação 31's thin-query hypothesis, Ação 40's broader confounds); fixed 31.1h and 40b/40d; full 46-Ação profile-completeness audit; clarified the "attractor chunk" terminology is a failure-mode metaphor, not a system mechanism:**
- **Trigger**: continuation of the 49th pass's diagnostic battery (`scratchpad/final_battery_test.py`, run across 7 Ações: 8,12,14,31,40,42,45) which had surfaced two new findings beyond that pass's original 15-Ação scope: Ação 40 confirmed still broken with a second confound type, and 31.1e/31.1h (never previously audited) confirmed vulnerable to the same attractor-chunk family already fixed at 31.1g. User then explicitly asked to see a PLAN before any Ação-31 changes, offering a diagnostic hypothesis (thin, phrase-heavy query terms starve the BM25 lane, letting the vector lane's attractor-chunk false positives dominate) and a smaller detail fix (Ação 40's "relação benefício-custo" uses reversed word order vs. this project's standard "custo-benefício" — add a NEAR term to catch both orders). Plan confirmed, executed, then re-tested via the full real `evaluate()` pipeline.
- **Query broadening for 31.1e/31.1g/31.1h + Ação 40's custo-benefício NEAR fix, retrieval-layer result**: confirmed via direct `retrieve_hybrid_candidates_for_acao()` inspection (no gate/LLM) that broadening **worked** for 31.1g — it now surfaces a genuine, on-topic BM25 hit (p.225, "exploração não poderá comprometer os padrões de qualidade dos SERVIÇOS") that didn't exist before. But 31.1e and 31.1h remained **100% vector-sourced with zero BM25 hits even after broadening** — confirming these two are genuine content gaps (the corpus has no literal text matching even the widened terms), not a fixable query-wording problem. The custo-benefício NEAR term for 40b produced no visible change in its own candidate pool (same generic outorga/receita content dominates regardless).
- **Full evaluate() battery after broadening (3 runs Ação 31, 2 runs Ação 40)**: 31.1g's real p.225 hit never made it into the gate's ACCEPTED set in this small sample — still dominated by known attractors (p.20/p.198, the "Anulação da concessão" region already confirmed as GPU/gate concurrency noise for other products). But this battery surfaced a **new, previously-unconfirmed finding**: 31.1h's own accepted evidence (`SMG-040_101607_2024_1.pdf` p.384/p.385, a "Bens Reversíveis" end-of-concession asset-maintenance/handover clause — flagged-but-never-verified since the 48th pass) was accepted in **3 of 3 runs**, a stability signature inconsistent with the intermittent GPU/gate concurrency-noise class and instead pointing to genuine content ambiguity per the Step 7.2 discriminator.
- **User asked me to clarify the "attractor chunk" terminology directly** (confirmed misunderstanding: not a "check neighboring chunks around an anchor" mechanism — no such mechanism exists anywhere in this pipeline, which evaluates each candidate chunk fully independently). Clarified: it is a purely empirical failure-mode metaphor for one specific piece of generic contractual boilerplate that (a) gets pulled into many unrelated products' candidate pools by the vector/semantic lane, because generic legal vocabulary (risco, responsabilidade, fiscalização, obrigação) superficially resembles many different IPMP criteria's own vocabulary, and (b) then fools the LLM gate into accepting it across those unrelated products, absent an explicit `negative_evidence_pattern` telling the gate to reject that specific class of content. Documented here since this distinction is easy to mis-read from the CLAUDE.md prose alone.
- **31.1h fixed.** Isolated 5x-sequential re-test (real GPU, exact production prompt) without any pattern: p.384 gave an unstable 1/5 (genuine ambiguity signature per the discriminator, not noise), p.385 gave a confident, uniform 5/5 (also genuine — the base prompt confidently, wrongly, accepts it every time, unlike the noise class which shows uniform *rejection* in isolation). A new `negative_evidence_pattern` (bens reversíveis / manutenção / desmobilização operacional / entrega de bens ao PODER CONCEDENTE at end-of-concession ≠ project-level quality-control procedures) brought both to a clean 0/5. **End-to-end confirmed** against the real `select_evidence()` pipeline (3 runs): p.384/385 never reappeared in any run — the only residual acceptances were single-occurrence hits in the already-known concurrency-noise region (p.198/p.199 for 31.1g/31.1h, p.336 for 31.1e, one each across 3 runs, none reproducible), left unfixed per the established "don't chase intermittent noise" precedent.
- **Ação 40 investigation-viability assessment, requested explicitly by the user before further work** ("verifique viabilidade... quais pros e contras... quero saber se realmente vale a pena investir tempo nisso"). Grounded via direct chunk-text inspection (not speculation) of all 14 previously-accepted chunks: **40b**'s entire accepted-evidence set (p.308-309, p.482-483, p.770-772) is **100% the original, static Estudo Econômico de Referência from Ação 18** (outorga/seguro/telefone tables, fluxo de caixa livre, EBITDA, IRPJ/CSLL, and the study's own "13 CONCLUSÃO" paragraph) — zero mention of consultation, percepção de mercado, or any post-consultation impact; the wrong artifact entirely, not a subtle stage confusion. **40c**'s sole intermittent hit (p.158) is an equipment technical-drawing page (MUPI dimensions/materials) — pure off-topic retrieval noise, not a content-ambiguity confound, and doesn't reproduce in every run. **40d** traces to **one root cause, not two**: the phrase "TERMO DE REFERÊNCIA" is this specific procurement's own base-document name, appearing on **167 of 1,402 indexed pages (12% of the whole corpus)** — both previously-seen variants (a Lei 14.133/2021 compliance checklist, and the contract's own Cláusula de Definições) are the same underlying false-friend, not two separate defects. Recommendation presented: fix 40b+40d (well-precedented technique, grounded diagnosis, high-value since 40 was the single most misleading full-credit score in the whole 15-Ação audit), skip 40c (intermittent, low-impact, not score-determining as currently observed). **User confirmed this scope.**
- **40b and 40d fixed**, one new `negative_evidence_pattern` each, both validated isolated 5x-sequential before applying: 40b's candidate pattern (original static economic-study content, pre-consultation, without any consultation-driven impact/reassessment framing, ≠ this product's required post-consultation impact analysis) took both a raw financial table and the study's own narrative conclusion from uniform 5/5 accepted to uniform 0/5. 40d's candidate pattern (TERMO DE REFERÊNCIA as this procurement's own document NAME ≠ a registry of data sources/references used in this revision) took the definições-clause chunk from 4/5 accepted (genuine ambiguity, not noise) to 0/5, while leaving the checklist chunk's already-clean 0/5-without-pattern behavior unchanged (consistent with that specific chunk's earlier live-battery acceptance having been concurrency noise, not a prompt defect — the pattern is kept anyway since it correctly generalizes across both real variants of the same root cause). **End-to-end confirmed dramatic correction**: before the fix, a live Ação-40 run gave `score=3, uncertainty=False, n_accepted=12` (entirely confound-driven, per the above). After the fix, 3 straight runs gave `score=0, uncertainty=True, n_accepted=1-2`, with the only accepted evidence being genuine 40a content (p.234, p.494) — 40b and 40d now correctly show **zero** accepted chunks in all 3 runs, meaning this Ação genuinely lacks real corpus evidence for those two products, a fact the confounds had been masking behind a false full-credit score.

Do the fix, than the push" — i.e. resolve Ação 22's "22.2" gap (flagged, not fixed, in the 44th pass) via an explicit schema field rather than continuing to infer scored-vs-header status from id shape, then commit and push.
- **`src/ingestion/ipmp.py`**: `ProdutoEsperado` gained `scored: bool = True`. False marks a non-scored section-header/container item; defaults True since most items are real scored products.
- **One-time data migration** (`scratchpad/add_scored_field.py`, not committed) added `"scored": false` to exactly the header items across `data/ipmp/acao_*.json` — computed via the same structural rule already used in code this session ("a product is a header iff some sibling id extends it"), with a single hardcoded exception: Ação 22's `"22.2"` is excluded from that rule (it stays `scored: true` by omission) since it's genuinely scored despite having scored children "22.2a"/"22.2b" that extend it — this is the exact case an id-shape rule alone can never resolve. **33 of the 46 files changed** (every Ação with a bare-number or dotted-numeric container header: 1-10, 12-15, 17-21, 24-25, 27, 30-36, 40-43; the other 13 — 11, 16, 22, 23, 26, 28-29, 37-39, 44-46 — have no header item at all, nothing to mark). Diffs are minimal (one added line per header item); re-serialization also silently fixed a stray trailing-space-before-comma artifact and a missing final newline in `acao_03.json`, both harmless.
- **`src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`**: the whole structural `any(other.startswith(product.id) ...)` heuristic from the 43rd pass is gone, replaced by a plain `if not product.scored: continue`. Docstrings updated (both `retrieve_hybrid_candidates_for_acao` and `retrieve_hybrid_for_acao`) to say "scored Expected Product" instead of the now-inaccurate "letter-suffixed Expected Product".
- **`src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py`**: both `build_system_prompt`'s `child_products` and `build_user_prompt`'s `product_order`/`product_texts` now filter on `p.scored` directly — the `parent_id`/`len(...) == 1` special-casing from the 43rd pass is gone.
- **`tests/test_hybrid_retrieval.py`**: `n_products` count now uses `p.scored` instead of `p.id[-1:].isalpha()`.
- **Verification**: fast suite 432/432 passing. A full cross-check of every profiled product across all 46 Ações (`profile.expected_products` vs. `ProdutoEsperado.scored`) now returns **zero mismatches** — Ação 22's `22.2` is confirmed scored=True alongside `22.1`/`22.2a`/`22.2b`, closing the gap the 44th pass's live GPU run had concretely observed (22.2 was named in the scoring prompt but never got its own retrieval query).
- **Committed and pushed** (commit message TBD at commit time) — `CLAUDE.md`, `src/ingestion/ipmp.py`, `src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`, `src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py`, `tests/test_hybrid_retrieval.py`, and all 46 `data/ipmp/acao_*.json` files (33 modified with the new field, the rest — many already pending from much earlier sessions as brand-new untracked files — committed for the first time as part of this same push, since a `scored` field can't be committed in isolation from a file that has never been committed at all). **Deliberately NOT included in this commit**: `data/retrieval_profiles/*.json` (untouched by this fix, and a much larger, separately-scoped body of multi-session retrieval-profile-synthesis work the user has not asked to commit) and the leftover `scripts/a3_inspect_retrieval_acao4.py`/`a4_inspect_evaluation_acao4.py` (unrelated stray files from an older session).

**Session 2026-09-07 (cont'd, 44th pass) — GPU-based Stage-B-style smoke test of the 43rd-pass schema fixes, real llama.cpp server, real SMG-040 documents — confirms both fixes work end-to-end, and concretely surfaces the practical consequence of the one residual gap (Ação 22's "22.2"):**
- **Direct-pipeline script** (`scratchpad/verify_schema_fix_gpu.py`, not committed — scratch only) ran `retrieve_document_focused` → `retrieve_hybrid_candidates_for_acao` → `select_evidence` → `evaluate()` against process `SMG-040_101607_2024` (1,429 indexed chunks) through the real llama.cpp GPU server (confirmed reachable, `/health` → 200) for two Ações chosen specifically to exercise the 43rd-pass fixes: **37** (bare-number sole product — previously entirely unloadable) and **22** (the dotted-numeric/dotted-letter hybrid structure with the one known, deliberately-unfixed residual classification gap).
- **Ação 37 — confirms root cause #1 (ipmp.py) and root cause #2 (hybrid.py) both fixed.** `ipmp loaded: True`, `produtos_esperados ids: ['37']`, `hybrid.py` correctly returned candidates keyed `['37']` (previously this Ação's file failed `AcaoIPMP.model_validate()` entirely — `get_ipmp_store()` never even reached the point of calling hybrid.py for it). The gate examined 8 candidates and rejected all 8 → `no_evidence_found=True`, `proposed_score=None` — a legitimate, unsurprising real-world outcome for a "seed"-maturity profile never validated against this specific document, not a pipeline defect. Worth a future Stage-B look: 5 of the 8 rejects carry non-empty `matched_concepts` (`judgment_and_selection_criteria_finalized` x3, `proposal_evaluation_governance`, `consolidated_finalized_document_set`) — topically-adjacent vocabulary the gate saw and declined, the standard "possible gate miss, review later" signal, not chased further now.
- **Ação 22 — confirms the hybrid.py structural fix works for 3 of 4 real scored products, and reproduces the documented "22.2" gap exactly as predicted, with a concrete consequence not previously spelled out.** `hybrid.py` returned candidates for `['22.1', '22.2a', '22.2b']` — **"22.2" itself never appears**, exactly matching the 43rd-pass code comment's prediction (it has scored children "22.2a"/"22.2b" that extend it, so the structural "no sibling extends this id" rule still classifies it as a container). The run completed successfully: 3 accepted / 25 rejected chunks, `proposed_score=3, uncertainty=False, no_evidence_found=False`. **New finding, not previously spelled out**: `build_system_prompt`'s scoring instructions still NAME "22.2" as one of the Produtos Esperados the LLM must check (its `child_products` list only excludes an item when its id equals the bare Ação number — "22.2" doesn't match "22" — so it survives that filter untouched), but no evidence chunk is ever retrieved specifically for it, since `hybrid.py` never issues a query for it at all. The LLM is effectively being asked to judge a criterion it was never given dedicated evidence for — it can only infer 22.2's status from whatever 22.1/22.2a/22.2b evidence happens to also be relevant, or from Documentos Focais. **This means an Ação-22 score should not be read as confirmation that 22.2's own specific expected content was actually checked** — a real, if narrow, correctness gap distinct from a simple missing-evidence case, since the auditor-facing prompt still implies 22.2 was considered. Flagged here, not fixed: resolving it cleanly needs either a schema-level `scored: bool` signal on `ProdutoEsperado` (touching all 46 user-authored IPMP files) or a narrower one-off carve-out for this single Ação — a decision left for the user, consistent with "corrections follow what Stage B reveals."
- **Conclusion: the 43rd-pass "investigate schema anomalies first" instruction and the original "faça o teste com a GPU que já está de pé" request are both now satisfied** — the loader bug (26/46 Ações silently invisible) and the retrieval-classifier bug (bare-number-sole-product and dotted-numeric-sibling-only products silently excluded) are fixed and confirmed working against the real GPU and real case documents; the one remaining gap (Ação 22's "22.2") is fully characterized, reproduced live, and explicitly left open for the user's call rather than force-fixed.
- **Fast test suite**: still 432/432 (unchanged since the 43rd pass — this pass only ran the live/GPU smoke script, no further code edits). **Nothing new is committed** — `src/ingestion/ipmp.py`, `src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`, `src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py` remain uncommitted from the 43rd pass; this pass added no repo changes, only a scratch verification script and this log entry.

**Session 2026-09-07 (cont'd, 43rd pass) — Schema anomaly investigation and CODE fixes (not data fixes) in `src/ingestion/ipmp.py`, `src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`, `src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py`; root-caused and fixed the reason 26 of 46 Ação files were silently invisible to the whole system:**
- **User instruction, disambiguated via `AskUserQuestion`**: after "Efetue as correções necessárias e faça o teste com a GPU que já está de pé," the user chose to **investigate schema anomalies first, before any GPU testing** ("Investigar as anomalias de schema primeiro"), with corrections scoped to "o que o Stage B revelar." GPU-based Stage B testing is deferred until this investigation is judged complete.
- **Root cause #1 — `get_ipmp_store()` was silently dropping Ações 21 through 46 (26 of 46 files) on every load, for the entire history of this session's Ação 21-46 work.** `src/ingestion/ipmp.py`'s `_load()` catches any `AcaoIPMP.model_validate()` failure, logs a `logger.warning`, and skips the file — no exception propagates, so nothing ever surfaced this. Confirmed via an instrumented diagnostic reload (`logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)`): before the fix, `Loaded 20/46 IPMP action files. Skipped: ['acao_21.json', ..., 'acao_46.json']`. Two independent validation failures, both present since Ação 21: (a) `fase: Literal["Inicial","Intermediária","Final"]` rejected the two newer phase values used from Ação 20/21 onward ("Aprimoramento da alternativa escolhida", "Consulta pública, Revisões e atualizações"); (b) `_ID_PATTERN = re.compile(r"^\d+[a-z]?$")` rejected every dotted-numeric product id ("22.1", "28.1", "31.1h", "46.1", ...). **Fixed** by widening both: `fase` Literal now includes all 5 observed values; `_ID_PATTERN` is now `r"^\d+(\.\d+)?[a-z]?$"`. Verified via the same diagnostic reload: `Loaded acao_ids: [1..46]`, `Count: 46`, `Missing: []`. This is a genuine **code** correction (the schema was too narrow for real, valid data), not a "flag but don't touch" data anomaly — the IPMP source JSON files themselves were not modified.
- **Root cause #2 — `src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`'s product-classification heuristic (`if not product.id[-1:].isalpha(): continue`, used to skip non-scored section-header items like "40" alongside "40a".."40d") was wrong for three of the newer id shapes**: a bare-number id that is the Ação's ONLY product (37, 38, 39, 44, 45 — genuinely scored, not a header, but its last character is a digit); a dotted-numeric-only id with no header at all (28.1/28.2/28.3, 29.1/29.2, 35.1/35.2, 46.1/46.2 — all genuinely scored); and Ação 22's "22.2" (genuinely scored AND itself the parent of scored children "22.2a"/"22.2b" — a case no id-shape rule can resolve, see below). **Fixed** by replacing the letter-suffix heuristic with a general structural rule, independent of retrieval-profile completeness (an earlier attempt to key off profile presence was tried and reverted — it broke `test_cascade_falls_back_per_product_when_missing_from_profile`, since a profile can legitimately cover only some of an Ação's products, with the rest meant to fall back to `build_bm25_query` on raw IPMP text, not be skipped as headers): a product is a non-scored container **iff some other product's id in the same Ação extends it** (`other.startswith(product.id)`). This correctly fixes the bare-number-sole-product and dotted-numeric-sibling-only cases without touching the classic lettered-suffix case at all.
- **Same root cause, same fix pattern, applied to `src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py`**: both `build_system_prompt`'s `child_products` list (feeds the scoring-instruction text naming which Produtos Esperados to check) and `build_user_prompt`'s `product_order`/`product_texts` (drives per-product evidence-block titling in the LLM prompt) used `p.id != parent_id` where `parent_id = str(acao_id)` — this incorrectly excluded the sole scored product for Ações 37/38/39/44/45, since there its id equals the bare Ação number with no sibling to distinguish it from a header. Fixed by adding a `len(acao.produtos_esperados) == 1` escape hatch to both call sites: the bare-number id is only excluded as a container when other products exist alongside it.
- **One residual, structurally-irresolvable gap confirmed and deliberately NOT force-fixed**: a cross-check of every profiled product across all 46 Ações (`profile.expected_products` vs. the new hybrid.py classifier) found exactly **one** mismatch — Ação 22's `"22.2"` is scored per its retrieval profile (density 4/12/9) but is still classified as a header by the new rule, because its own children "22.2a"/"22.2b" extend it — this was ALSO already broken under the pre-fix code (its last character is a digit, so the old `isalpha()` check skipped it too), so this is not a new regression, just the one case the general rule cannot resolve without an explicit schema-level signal (e.g. a `scored: bool` field on `ProdutoEsperado`, which does not currently exist and was not added — adding it would mean touching all 46 already-user-authored IPMP JSON files, judged out of scope for a heuristic bug-fix pass and left for the user to decide on, consistent with "corrections follow what Stage B reveals"). Every other one of the 46 Ações' profiled products now classifies correctly.
- **Fast test suite**: 432/432 passing after both fixes (an intermediate profile-membership-based attempt at the hybrid.py fix was tried, caused exactly one regression, and was reverted before landing on the final structural rule above).
- **Not yet done**: GPU-based Stage B testing (deferred per the user's explicit sequencing) — to follow now that this schema investigation is complete. The remaining flagged-but-unaddressed *data* anomalies from the 42nd-pass entry below (the untagged "termos de referência" collision between Ações 7/23, the duplicated "33"/"34" header text, the `ponto_transicao` boolean-vs-prose contradiction at Ações 37/38/45/46) are unrelated to this code fix and still unaddressed. Nothing from this pass is committed yet — `src/ingestion/ipmp.py`, `src/retrieval/query/hybrid.py`, and `src/evaluation/prompt/builder.py` all have uncommitted changes, joining the long-standing pending queue.

Fixed reliably by writing a standalone script file (`scratchpad/fix_acao21_json.py`) via the `Write` tool and executing it via a plain `Bash` call with no embedded complex string content — succeeded immediately, escaping the raw newlines to `\n` in place. (One apparent "A??o" mojibake seen mid-diagnosis in inline-Bash `repr()` output was verified via direct `Read` to be a pure terminal display artifact, not real file corruption — the on-disk UTF-8 content was correct throughout.) **New standing technical note**: on this Windows/Git-Bash setup, prefer a standalone `.py` script file (via `Write`) over embedding non-ASCII or heavily-escaped string logic directly in an inline `Bash` command.

Density deliberately uneven: 19a=2/8/5, 19b=4/11/9 (densest — carries most of the Ação's por_que_importante/o_que_esperar/excecoes weight: risk identification, licensing/land risk, stakeholder perspective, resettlement social impact), 19c=2/4/3, 19d=1/3/2 (concepts/terms/active).
- **A new `shared_with_acao` relationship subtype: explicit, IPMP-text-stated COMPLEMENTARY/gap-filling coverage, distinct from every escalation/confound/dependency pattern documented so far.** `o_que_esperar` states outright: "Identificação e categorização de riscos que ainda não tenham sido capturados na Ação 18 ou que demandem avaliação qualitativa adicional." Ação 18b performs the QUANTIFIED/monetized valuation (TSD, viés de otimismo, custo de risco, Valor Público); Ação 19a/19b exist specifically to capture the non-monetizable residue quantification can't reach. This is a polarity split of the same underlying activity, not a maturity escalation — tagged `shared_with_acao:[18]` on 19a/19b's core terms WITH a negative_evidence_pattern (unlike the purely-positive 18a→6 tag), since an 18b-style quantified chunk could otherwise be mistaken for satisfying 19's qualitative bar.
- **The project's largest recurring confound field — licenciamento ambiental/desapropriação/remoção de interferências — grows from 6-way (Ações 4,5,7,8,12,15) to a 7-way convergence, its largest yet.** `excecoes` #2 states outright to consider these exact risks; Ação 19's own sense is the QUALITATIVE deliberative judgment ("quanto se está disposto a pagar para mitigá-lo") within a benefit-risk trade-off — distinct from all six prior senses (catalog/FCS-criterion/AIAS-scoping/contractual-survey/schedule-tracking/structured-register). Tagged `shared_with_acao:[4,5,7,8,12,15]` on 19b's bare "licenciamento ambiental" (kept experimental, mirroring the 15a/15c precedent) and the qualified NEAR anchor (kept active).

Tagged `shared_with_acao:[4,5]`. (2) "famílias a serem reassentadas"/reassentamento vocabulary — a three-way collision (5b FCS criterion, 7a AIAS risk-scoping, 13a stakeholder identification) — gains a fourth sense: 19b's qualitative SOCIAL-IMPACT assessment of resettlement itself (desintegração social, perda de laços comunitários), reflecting this Ação's own Atendido example (Programa Habitacional). Tagged `shared_with_acao:[5,7,13]`.
- **New link to Ação 13, distinct from the reassentamento thread.** `excecoes` #5 requires incorporating "a perspectiva de diferentes grupos de partes interessadas" — Ação 13a identifies WHO the stakeholders are; Ação 19b requires incorporating THEIR VIEWPOINT into the qualitative judgment itself, a distinct evaluative act. Ação 6c's "análise de custo-benefício"/"ACB preliminar" is a BRIEF, qualitative, options-narrowing comparison (no monetary quantification, no discount rate, no optimism-bias adjustment) between still-competing alternatives; Ação 10a's "estimativa de custos" is an INITIAL CAPEX/OPEX/maintenance estimate per alternative feeding a funding-capacity assessment (dimensão Financeira); Ação 18b's own cost/benefit analysis is the DETAILED, fully monetized version (TSD, viés de otimismo, custo de risco, Valor Público, RCB) for the options that survived Ação 6's narrowing. Tagged `shared_with_acao:[6]` on 18b's "análise de custo-benefício" term (active) and `shared_with_acao:[6,10]` on the bare "estimativa de custos" phrase (kept experimental given the 3-way collision risk — the qualified "custos detalhados por categoria" is the active term instead), each with a matching negative_evidence_pattern excluding a bare Ação-6c-level preliminary sketch as insufficient evidence for 18b's own detailed-maturity bar.

Tagged `shared_with_acao:[14]` on 18b's "Benefícios Públicos" term with a matching negative_evidence_pattern excluding a bare categorized-benefit list lacking any monetary estimate.

Re-read `data/ipmp/acao_04.json` in full to confirm — 4b's risk catalog (viabilidade técnica, aceitação social, ambiental, político-econômico) is a genuine textual precursor. Tagged `shared_with_acao:[4]` on 15a's "riscos potenciais" term, distinguishing 4b's narrative strategic-risk description (embedded in the EVTEA, evaluated without impact/probability scoring, mitigation ownership, or a matrix structure) from 15's dedicated, structured risk-management register (which 15b-15f build out with impact/probability, mitigation, escalation, party-allocation and ownership) — same "narrative vs. structured tracking document" relationship already used for 14a↔4a.
- **The single largest recurring confound field in this project — licenciamento ambiental/desapropriação/remoção de interferências — reaches its sixth and most complete member here, closing the loop this session has tracked since Ação 4b.** A broad cross-Ação grep (pattern: licenciamento|desapropria|interferênc|matriz de risco|risco compartilhado|risco da contratante|risco da contratada|riscos ambientais|riscos sistêmicos|riscos estratégicos) matched 10 existing profiles; individual `-C 3` follow-up greps against each confirmed five prior distinct senses already documented this session: **4b** (strategic risk catalog, a product in itself), **5b** (FCS cost/schedule-impact comparison criterion between alternatives), **7b** (AIAS scoping — which studies/TRs are needed), **8d** (preliminary contractual/institutional risk survey before choosing the contracting model), **12a** (schedule/milestone-tracking sense, explicitly called the "sixth distinct sense" in that file's own negative pattern — a slight numbering quirk now superseded by this file's own count). Ação 15a/15c is the sixth and fullest sense yet: actual RISK IDENTIFICATION plus MITIGATION within a dedicated preliminary risk register, exactly the emphasis Ação 15's own `excecoes` #3 calls for ("dar ênfase na gestão de riscos que envolvam os processos de licenciamento ambiental, de remoção de interferências e de desapropriação"). Tagged `shared_with_acao:[4,5,7,8,12]` on 15a's "desafios de obtenção de licenças" (active) and bare "licenciamento ambiental" (kept experimental given the 6-way collision risk) terms, and on 15c's licenciamento/desapropriação/interferências NEAR term, each with a shared_with_acao_note distinguishing all five prior senses from 15's own identification+mitigation sense — no negative_evidence_pattern needed here since 15 is explicitly meant to build on 4b's content, not exclude it.

Ação 15e ("Delimitação simples dos riscos da contratante, da contratada e o risco compartilhado") is precisely that content — confirmed, via this Ação's own `por_que_importante` ("o que se espera não é uma matriz definitiva, mas sim uma estrutura preliminar coerente com o estágio do projeto"), to be only a preliminary sketch of it, not the definitive post-signing contractual annex 1a/2c/2d were guarding against. They're retired (won't be run again) but kept as-is, unmodified — their structure is the reference pattern for writing the next Ação's `a3`/`a4_..._acaoNN.py` pair. See [[legacy-inspection-scripts-are-generation-templates]] memory.

Validated against the real pipeline (extract→index→cascade→gate→evaluate, real llama.cpp) in an isolated temp DB (scratchpad, not committed): **Score3 → score=3, uncertainty=False (exact match)**; **Score1 → score=1, uncertainty=False (exact match)**. **Score0 → `no_evidence_found=True`/`score=None`, not literally `0`** — this surfaced a real architectural nuance, not a corpus bug: `EvaluationResult`'s invariant makes `score=0` mean "evidence was accepted and judged insufficient," while `no_evidence_found=True` means "the gate accepted nothing at all" — a fully off-topic engineering-works memorial (first draft of the Score0 doc) hits the latter, not the former. Revised the doc to add a vague project-purpose paragraph mirroring the IPMP's own "Não Atendido" worked example (topically present, but no real objectives/indicators/diagnosis) — this pushed it onto the gate's decision boundary (`matched_concepts` now appear on rejected candidates: `performance_indicators`, `expected_results`, `gap_analysis`), and its accept/reject outcome now varies run-to-run — confirmed by re-running twice (0/8 then 1/8 accepted) — consistent with, not a new instance of, the already-documented gate/GPU non-determinism. Deliberately not chased further: forcing a specific deterministic outcome here would mean doing the deferred gate investigation early.
- **NEW — gate/GPU instability investigation:** pending, explicitly ordered to run after Componente 5. Context to resume from: sequential single-slot gate calls are stable; 4-way concurrency on an identical prompt flips verdicts (6/20 in one probe) and can corrupt a response; per-slot testing disproved "one bad slot" as the cause — different slots diverge on different borderline prompts, and even a single slot can self-flip on back-to-back identical calls with zero concurrency; no deterministic-kernel flag exists in the current llama.cpp build (PR #16016 unmerged/draft); `cache_prompt:false` gave mixed, inconclusive results on a small sample. User asked to review the model's full `reasoning_content` per case/slot (artifact published, see above) before choosing between majority-vote across N calls, reduced gate concurrency, or another approach — that review was still pending when this plan was adopted.
  **RESOLVED 2026-08-27 — see next session entry below.**

**Session 2026-08-27 — negative_evidence_patterns wiring + gate/GPU investigation resolved (root cause: content, not hardware):**
- Wired the previously-dormant `negative_evidence_patterns` field (`ExpectedProductProfile`, existed since ADR-0047 but never read) into the gate prompt (`evaluation/prompt/relevance_builder.py`, `evaluation/evidence_selection.py`). Scoped per-Ação/per-product via `select_evidence(acao_id, ...)` → `get_retrieval_profile_store().acoes.get(acao_id)` — no shared/canonical list, no hardcoded Ação-1 path (explicitly verified in code before applying, given the project's past experience with prompts hardcoded to Ação 1). Ação 1 already had patterns populated (1a, 1b, 1c, 1d) that had never been exercised by the gate; Ação 2 gained 2 new (2c: sanctions clauses, 2d: risk matrix). Commit `0523442`.
- **Gate/GPU instability investigation: resolved for the known target cases.** 704 real gate calls direct to the llama.cpp primary (bypassing `FallbackLLMClient`) across per-slot/sequential/pairs/4-concurrent homogeneous batteries (`scratchpad/run_4_batteries.py`) plus heterogeneous real-product-mix batteries (`run_heterogeneous_battery{,_v2,_v3}.py`) isolated the root cause: **content ambiguity, not GPU/kernel non-determinism alone.** Every homogeneous-concurrency test (same prompt vs. itself — any single slot, sequential, pairs, 4-concurrent) was 100% stable (0/184) for all 4 known-unstable candidates (1a/pg195, 1d/pg235, 2c/pg302, 2d/pg194). Only heterogeneous concurrency — 4 *different* products' prompts sharing GPU slots at once, matching production's actual `select_evidence()` `ThreadPoolExecutor` shape — reproduced flips, and only for 1a (2/40 baseline, n=50 combined). Capturing full `reasoning_content` on all 3 observed flips showed they are not random: every one hinges on the identical phrase ("Danos advindos de vício ou defeito nos bens vinculados à CONCESSÃO"), with the model inconsistently treating a contractual risk/responsibility-matrix item (who bears the cost if something breaks) as an operational-deficiency diagnosis (the actual 1a criterion). `cache_prompt: false` (a llama.cpp maintainer's suggested workaround for cross-request KV-cache contamination, from the prior session's research — PR #16016 "deterministic inference mode" is still unmerged/draft, no kernel-level fix available) was tested at matched n=40 under the specific condition that reproduces the flip and discarded: reduced but did not eliminate it (2/40→1/40, not statistically distinguishable from noise at this sample size).
- **Fix applied:** added a 4th `negative_evidence_pattern` to Ação 1 / 1a (`data/retrieval_profiles/acao_01.json`) naming the exact confound. Judged scalable before applying (explicit user criterion: discard fixes that only help one Ação or don't reproduce across the other 45) — this is the 3rd occurrence this session of the same failure class (risk/responsibility-allocation boilerplate vs. a diagnosis-type criterion: 2c, 2d, now 1a), and risk-matrices are near-mandatory content in Brazilian PPP contracts (Lei 11.079/2004), colliding with the "diagnóstico da situação atual" criterion shape several of the 46 IPMP actions share — judged a structural domain confound, not an Ação-1 peculiarity. Confirmatory battery at the exact baseline condition (heterogeneous, n=40, default `cache_prompt`): 1a 2/40 → **0/40**, other 3 cases unchanged at 0/40. 432/432 fast tests passing throughout (JSON-only change). Commit `8b02421`, pushed.
- Full forensic report, updated in place across all 4 rounds of testing: `gate_concurrency_forensics.html` artifact (not in this repo — published to claude.ai).
- **Open/residual:** 2c/pg302's historical "7/10 accepted" seen earlier the same day under the full production pipeline (`run_10_instrumented_gate.py`, different candidate mix examined per run) was not reproduced by the heterogeneous battery's fixed-4-candidate-repeat design (0/90 combined for 2c across all conditions tested) — the two designs aren't strictly equivalent (production varies which candidates are examined per run, this harness always repeats the same 4). Worth a full live E2E re-check if 2c resurfaces in a real assessment run, rather than assuming it's fully closed.
- Also this session (before the above): 2 live E2E checkpoint tests against real SMG-040 documents post-Componente-5, non-regression on Ação 1 confirmed stable (score=1 across ~40+ runs this session).

Does not affect post-gate dedup (threshold=0.70).

Binary relevant/not-relevant only — no LLM cleaning (dropped; `evaluation/evidence_selection.py::strip_extraction_noise()` is the only cleaning now, deterministic). `_MAX_EXAMINED=8` candidates examined per product; per-product gating runs concurrently via `ThreadPoolExecutor`. Measured: ~20.6 min for a full Ação 1 assessment on the reference CPU-only hardware (6-core/12-thread, no usable GPU) — down from 68 min pre-fix, but still far from "all 46 actions in one interactive sitting" (~15.8 hours extrapolated). **ADR-0051: interactive assessments are scoped to one Ação today, a small batch (3-4) once Phase 1 expands — never a full 46-action run in one sitting.** Smaller local models (`LiquidAI/LFM2.5-350M`) were tested and rejected (blanket-accept failure, not viable at that size for this task).

1. ✅ Retrieval profiles — 1b updated with 5 real-document terms (marked experimental to avoid BM25 TOC ranking)
2. ✅ Pre-gate semantic dedup (`_prefilter_near_duplicates`, threshold=0.92)
3. ✅ Regex retrieval REMOVED from cascade (ADR-0052) — Step D dropped entirely. Restores pg=211 (1c strategic metas) to every evidence set by eliminating the cap displacement. `search_regex()` preserved as utility.
4. ✅ Deterministic concept attribution (`_attribute_concepts`) — `matched_concepts` on both `RetrievedChunk` and `RejectedChunk`
5. ✅ `build_query_from_terms` filters experimental/deprecated terms — BM25 retrieval only uses active/validated terms

**Key architectural lessons from this work:**
- `build_query_from_terms` now filters by status (active/validated only) — experimental terms excluded from BM25 but included in concept attribution
- Experimental terms in retrieval profiles = "registered for attribution and future validation, not yet trusted for retrieval"
- `_prefilter_near_duplicates` runs in production `select_evidence()` but live_report.py must also call it for accurate representation
- Ollama KV cache can produce identical test output across runs for identical prompts — careful comparison of candidate rankings (not just gate verdicts) is needed to verify retrieval changes

**Latency:** ~17-20 min/Ação (ADR-0051 scope: one Ação at a time). Pre-gate dedup saves ~6 gate calls (3 for 1b conclusion copies, 3 for 1d boilerplate). Further reduction requires non-LLM pre-filter or fine-tuned small model (deferred).

**Tests (2026-07-01):** 386 fast + 12 slow = 398 total (2026-10-19: 757 fast). Run with `pytest -m "not slow"` for fast path.

Install with: `SETUPTOOLS_USE_DISTUTILS=stdlib pip install -e .`
Run tests: `pytest -m "not slow"` (fast) or `pytest` (includes OCR; requires `TESSERACT_CMD` env var).

**Module 5 design decisions (grill-me 2026-06-02):**
- ADR-0022: `AssessmentService` owns orchestration; FastAPI is presentation layer
- ADR-0023: Review Outcome contract — accept/override; `final_score` always in {0,1,3}; `justification` required on override
- ADR-0024: Shared SQLite DB, schema-level table ownership per module
- ADR-0025: `EvaluationResult` persistence — hybrid (normalised columns + `raw_json` blob)
- ADR-0026: Replacement lifecycle; no versioning; app-layer 409 guard; explicit DELETE+INSERT (no INSERT OR REPLACE)
- ADR-0027: 5 Phase 1 routes; multipart file upload; synchronous execution
- ADR-0028: PDFs are transient; `chunks` table is canonical extracted artifact store; no PDF storage
- ADR-0029: SHA-256 fingerprint-based reuse/replacement; `document_fingerprints` table owned by Module 5
- ADR-0030: Assessment scope from `get_ipmp_store()`, not hardcoded; aggregation deferred (built on read in ADR-0075, 2026-10-15)
- ADR-0031: `EvaluationResult.model_dump()` as direct HTTP response; no shaped DTOs for reads
- ADR-0032: Independent `configure(db_path)` per module; `main.py` owns startup sequencing

**Module sequence (deep modules — one complete before the next):**
1. Ingestion (`src/ingestion/`) ← **COMPLETE**
2. PDF extraction + chunking (`src/extraction/`) ← **COMPLETE**
3. BM25 retrieval (`src/retrieval/`) ← **COMPLETE** (issues #11–15)
4. LLM evaluation (`src/evaluation/`) ← **COMPLETE** (issues #16–20)
5. Auditor review interface (`src/assessment/` + `main.py`) ← **COMPLETE** (issues #21–25)
6. Vector retrieval (LanceDB) ← **COMPLETE** (a fallback in ADR-0033; co-equal and always-on since ADR-0049)
7. Frontend (Vue.js 3) ← **COMPLETE** (served by FastAPI from `frontend/dist`)

**Key reference files:**
- `docs/prd_module_04_evaluation.md` — Module 4 PRD (36 user stories, GitHub issues #16–20)
- `docs/prd_module_05_assessment.md` — Module 5 PRD (35 user stories, GitHub issues #21–25)
- `CONTEXT.md` — canonical domain glossary (last updated 2026-10-15: Nota iPMP, Fase, Ponto de Transição)
- `docs/adr/` — 78 ADRs, next is `0079-`
- `docs/dan/` — Deferred Architecture Notes (both closed), next is `0003-`
- `docs/handoffs/retrieval-profile-design-2026-06-17.md` — full design session handoff (evidence ontology, schema, population methodology)
- `data/ipmp/acao_NN.json` — IPMP source of truth, one file per Ação (46)
- `data/rio_manual/acao_01.json`, `acao_02.json` — Rio Manual retrieval context (Ações 1 and 2 only)

## Environment

- Conda env: `rel_voto` (Python 3.11)
- `pip-system-certs` required for HuggingFace downloads through corporate proxy
- Run scripts via `python` directly (not `conda run`) — avoids Windows UTF-8 errors
- Set `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` before running scripts
- Set `TESSERACT_CMD=C:\Users\sanseri\AppData\Local\Programs\Tesseract-OCR\tesseract.exe` — conda subprocess does not inherit User-level PATH; set in `.claude/settings.local.json`
- HuggingFace model cached at `C:\Users\sanseri\.cache\huggingface\hub\`
- Set `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` and `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` before running anything that calls `retrieval/query/vector.py` (i.e. any real assessment run) — a newer `transformers`/`huggingface_hub` probes for an optional `adapter_config.json` on huggingface.co even for a fully-cached, non-adapter model, and that probe fails with `SSL: CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED` behind the corporate proxy (confirmed 2026-08-25, `pip-system-certs`'s truststore injection doesn't cover it). The offline flags skip the network probe entirely and use the local cache, which already has the model.

## Methodology

### ADR (`docs/adr/`)

Sequential numbering: `0001-slug.md`. Create directory lazily — only when the first ADR is needed.

**Template:**
```
# {Short title}
{1-3 sentences: context, decision, why.}
```

**Optional sections** (only when they add genuine value):
- `Status` frontmatter: `proposed | accepted | deprecated | superseded by ADR-NNNN`
- `Considered Options` — when rejected alternatives are worth remembering
- `Consequences` — when non-obvious downstream effects need calling out

**Numbering:** scan `docs/adr/` for the highest number, increment by one.

**When to create — all three must be true:**
1. Hard to reverse
2. Surprising without context — a future reader would wonder "why on earth did they do it this way?"
3. Result of a real trade-off — genuine alternatives existed

**What qualifies:** architectural shape, technology choices with lock-in, boundary/scope decisions (including explicit exclusions), deliberate deviations from the obvious path, constraints not visible in code, rejected alternatives where the rejection is non-obvious.

---

### DAN (`docs/dan/`)

Deferred Architecture Notes capture strategy spaces that are too important to lose but intentionally unresolved. Unlike ADRs (sealed decisions), DANs are living documents that evolve across phases.

**Use a DAN when:**
- A design area has stable directives but open trade-offs not ready to become ADRs
- Known complexity is intentionally deferred (with documented reasons)
- Multiple future triggers may change the approach independently

**Numbering:** sequential `0001-slug.md` in `docs/dan/`.

**Template:**
```
# DAN-{NNNN} — {Subsystem}: Deferred Architecture

**Status:** Active | Closed (decisions moved to ADR-XXXX)
**Related ADR:** (if any)
**Last updated:** {date}

## Stable Directives       ← do not revisit unless a trigger fires
## Known Constraints       ← hard requirements the module must satisfy
## Deferred Complexity Areas  ← table of deferred problems + why
## Candidate Tooling Ecosystem  ← research directions, not commitments (optional)
## Non-Goals for Phase N   ← explicit exclusions
## Future Triggers         ← events that require revisiting this DAN
```

**When to close a DAN:** mark `Status: Closed` and link the ADRs that resolved the deferred areas. Do not delete — closed DANs explain why certain ADRs exist.

---

### CONTEXT.md (root)

Single-context repo — one `CONTEXT.md` at root.

**Template:**
```
# {Context Name}
{One or two sentence description.}

## Language
**Term**: One-sentence definition of what it IS.
_Avoid_: rejected synonyms

## Relationships
- **Term A** produces one or more **Term B**s

## Example dialogue
> Dev/domain-expert conversation showing terms interacting naturally.

## Flagged ambiguities
- Term used ambiguously — resolved: clear resolution.
```

**Rules:**
- Be opinionated: pick one canonical name, list rejected synonyms under _Avoid_.
- One sentence per definition. Define what it IS, not what it does.
- Only project-specific concepts — not general programming terms.
- Show relationships with cardinality where obvious.
- Always write an example dialogue.
- Flag and resolve any term used ambiguously.
- **CONTEXT.md is a glossary only.** No implementation details, specs, or scratch-pad notes.

---

### grill-me

Prompt file: `docs/prompts/01_grill-me.md`.
to-PRD skill: `docs/prompts/02_To-PRD.md`.
IPMP reference PDF: `docs/IPMP TCU2026 - Indicador_percepcao_maturidade.pdf`.

Use before designing each new module to stress-test the plan against the domain model and documented decisions. Updates CONTEXT.md and ADRs inline as decisions crystallise — do not batch.

During a grill-me session: challenge terminology against CONTEXT.md, sharpen fuzzy language, stress-test with concrete scenarios, cross-reference with code when relevant.
